Let $\Gamma_1$ and $\Gamma_2$ be connected quivers with $D := \partial\Gamma_1^+ = \partial\Gamma_2^- \neq \emptyset.$ Then there is a canonical isomorphism of complex Hamiltonian $G_{\mathbb{C}}^{\partial\Gamma_1^-} \times G_{\mathbb{C}}^{\partial\Gamma_2^+}$-spaces:
\[
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Gamma_1 *_D \Gamma_2) \cong \left( \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Gamma_1) \times \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Gamma_2) \right) /\!\!/\!\!/_{\mathbb{C}}\, G_{\mathbb{C}}^D,
\]
where the reduction is taken with respect to the moment map $\mu_{\Delta, D} = \mu_{\mathrm{out}}^{(1)} - \mu_{\mathrm{in}}^{(2)}.$
<!-- end prop-18 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
This follows immediately from Theorem \ref{thm:finite-dim-reduction} and reduction by stages.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- def-9: body_md -->
\label{def:homotopic}
An \textbf{\textit{edge contraction}} of a quiver $\Gamma$ with boundary removes an edge $e_0$ whose endpoints are two \emph{distinct interior} vertices $v_1 = s(e_0) \neq v_2 = t(e_0)$ and identifies $v_1$ with $v_2$ (Figure \ref{fig:homotopy}); its inverse is called an \textbf{\textit{edge expansion}}. Two quivers $\Gamma_{1}$ and $\Gamma_2$ are \textbf{\textit{homotopic}} if one can be obtained from the other by a finite sequence of edge contractions and edge expansions.
<!-- end def-9 -->
<!-- prop-19: body_md -->
[Invariance under homotopy moves]
\label{thm:spine-invariance}
Let $\Gamma$ and $\Gamma_1$ be two homotopic oriented quivers. Then, there exists a canonical isomorphism of complex Hamiltonian spaces
\[
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Gamma) \cong \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Gamma_1).
\]
Consequently, the isomorphism class of $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Sigma) := \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Gamma)$ depends only on the underlying cobordism $\Sigma$.
<!-- end prop-19 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
This follows from the length-independence of $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Gamma)$    (Proposition \ref{prop:length-invariance-complex}) and the proof of \cite[\text{Theorem 6.4}]{maiza2026lax}.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- thm-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:2d-tqft-functor}

There exists a unique symmetric monoidal functor
\[
\mathcal{Z} : \mathbf{Cob}_2 \longrightarrow \mathbf{Ham}_{\mathbb{C}}
\]
defined by:
\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{On Objects:} For $n \ge 0$ disjoint circles,
    \[
    \mathcal{Z}\left(\bigsqcup^n S^1\right) := G_{\mathbb{C}}^n, \qquad \mathcal{Z}(\emptyset) := \{*\}.
    \]
    \item \textbf{On Morphisms:} For any cobordism $\Sigma : \bigsqcup^m S^1 \to \bigsqcup^n S^1$ represented by an oriented quiver with boundary $\Gamma$,
    \[
    \mathcal{Z}(\Sigma) := \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Gamma),
    \]
    equipped with its descended holomorphic symplectic structure and boundary evaluation moment map $(\mu_{\mathrm{in}}, \mu_{\mathrm{out}}) : \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Gamma) \to (\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{C}}^*)^m \times (\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{C}}^*)^n$.
\end{itemize}
<!-- end thm-7 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
The proof follows the same steps as the proofs of \cite[\text{Theorem 7.4}]{maiza2026multiplicative} and \cite[\text{Theorem 7.2}]{maiza2026lax}.
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- prop-20: body_md -->
\label{prop:cylinder-length}
Assume $\dim G > 0$.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item The zero fibre $Z_L \coloneqq (\mu^{\partial})^{-1}(0) \subset \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_e,L)$ is the set of classes $[A]$ with $A_1 = A_2 = A_3 = 0$. It is a single $G\times G$-orbit, and the holonomy map $Z_L \to G$ is a diffeomorphism which is an isometry from the induced metric onto $(G, L^{-1}g_{\mathrm{bi}})$.
    \item For $L\neq L'$, the spaces $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_e,L)$ and $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_e,L')$ are not isomorphic as hyperkähler Hamiltonian $G\times G$-spaces, i.e.\ there is no $G\times G$-equivariant hyperkähler isometry between them intertwining the moment maps.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end prop-20 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
(1) Let $[A]\in Z_L$, so that $A_a(0) = A_a(L) = 0$ for $a = 1,2,3$. With $\alpha = A_0 + iA_1$ and $\beta = A_2 + iA_3$, the complex Nahm equation $\dot{\beta} + [\alpha,\beta] = 0$ gives $\beta(t) = \operatorname{Ad}_{g_\alpha(t)}\beta(0) = 0$, so $A_2 = A_3 = 0$. The first Nahm equation then reads $\dot{A}_1 + [A_0,A_1] = 0$, so $A_1$ is parallel and $A_1(0) = 0$ forces $A_1 \equiv 0$. Conversely, every $A = (A_0,0,0,0)$ solves Nahm's equations (there are no interior vertices). Two connections $A_0, A'_0$ on $[0,L]$ are related by a gauge transformation trivial at both ends if and only if they have the same holonomy $h = g_{A_0}(L)$, and $(g_0,g_1)\in G\times G$ acts on holonomies by $h\mapsto g_1hg_0^{-1}$. Hence the holonomy map $Z_L\to G$ is a $G\times G$-equivariant bijection onto a homogeneous space, so $Z_L$ is a single orbit of a compact group, hence a compact embedded submanifold, and the holonomy map is a diffeomorphism.

To compute the induced metric, note that since $G$ is compact and connected, every $h\in G$ is of the form $\exp(-L\xi)$ for some $\xi\in\mathfrak{g}$, so every point of $Z_L$ is represented by a constant $A_0\equiv\xi$. The horizontal tangent vectors to $Z_L$ at $[A]$ are the $Y = (Y_0,0,0,0)$ with $D^{*}_AY = \dot{Y}_0 + [\xi,Y_0] = 0$ (there are no vertex terms, and the orbit directions $D_Au$ also have vanishing Higgs components), that is, $Y_0(t) = \operatorname{Ad}_{\exp(-t\xi)}c$ for some $c\in\mathfrak{g}$, with $\|Y\|^2_{L^2} = L\|c\|^2$. Writing the variation of the holonomy as $\delta g_{A_0}(t) = g_{A_0}(t)\eta(t)$ with $g_{A_0}(t) = \exp(-t\xi)$, the linearized transport equation gives $\dot{\eta} = -g_{A_0}^{-1}Y_0\,g_{A_0} = -c$, so $\eta(L) = -Lc$ and $\|\delta h\|_{g_{\mathrm{bi}}} = L\|c\|$. Therefore the induced metric is $\|\delta h\|^2_{g_{\mathrm{bi}}}/L$, as claimed.

(2) An isomorphism $f$ of hyperkähler Hamiltonian $G\times G$-spaces satisfies $\mu'^{\partial}\circ f = \mu^{\partial}$, hence maps $Z_L$ onto $Z_{L'}$ by a Riemannian isometry. By (1), $\mathrm{vol}(Z_L) = L^{-\dim G/2}\,\mathrm{vol}_{g_{\mathrm{bi}}}(G)$, which differs from $\mathrm{vol}(Z_{L'})$ when $L\neq L'$ because $\dim G > 0$; contradiction.
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- rem-10: body_md -->
\label{rem:obstruction}
One might ask whether the hyperkähler moduli spaces $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma)$ assemble into a 2D TQFT valued in a hyperkähler category whose morphisms are hyperkähler Hamiltonian manifolds composed by hyperkähler reduction $/\!\!/\!\!/_{\mathbb{H}}$, by setting $\Sigma\mapsto \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma)$ for any spine $\Gamma$ of $\Sigma$.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item This assignment is not well defined on $\mathbf{Cob}_2$: the cylinder admits the spines $(\Gamma_e,L)$ for every $L > 0$, and by Proposition \ref{prop:cylinder-length} the corresponding hyperkähler spaces are pairwise non-isomorphic.
    \item The obstruction is due entirely to the total length of the edges: by Theorem \ref{thm:hklr-metric-gluing}, subdividing an edge into two sub-edges whose lengths add up to the original length is a hyperkähler isometry. It is the total length, which $\mathbf{Cob}_2$ does not record, that changes the hyperkähler isomorphism class.
    \item By Proposition \ref{prop:metric-length-dependence}(1), a uniform rescaling of all lengths acts by a homothety. Hence the obstruction for the cylinder disappears if hyperkähler spaces are only considered up to homothety. For general spines, Proposition \ref{prop:metric-length-dependence}(2) shows that the transported metrics $g^{X}_{\mathbf{l}}$ are not related by the canonical identifications; we make no claim about the existence of other identifications.
\end{enumerate}
This motivates retaining the lengths as part of the data, which we do in Section \ref{section 6}.
<!-- end rem-10 -->
<!-- def-10: body_md -->
The non-unital monoidal category of metric 2-cobordisms, denoted $\mathbf{MetCob}_2$, is defined as follows:
\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{Objects:} Finite disjoint unions of metric circles
    \[
    \bigsqcup_{i=1}^n (S^1, L_i), \qquad L_i \in \mathbb{R}_{>0},
    \]
    where $L_i$ denotes the assigned metric perimeter of the $i$-th circle. The empty union $\emptyset$ serves as the monoidal unit.
    \item \textbf{Morphisms:} A morphism from $\bigsqcup_{i=1}^m (S^1, L_i^{\mathrm{in}})$ to $\bigsqcup_{j=1}^n (S^1, L_j^{\mathrm{out}})$ is an equivalence class of triples $(\Sigma, \Gamma, \mathbf{l})$, where:
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item $\Sigma$ is a smooth compact oriented 2-manifold with boundary decomposed as $\partial\Sigma = \overline{\Sigma}_{\mathrm{in}} \sqcup \Sigma_{\mathrm{out}};$
        \item $\Gamma = (E, V, s, t)$ is an embedded quiver retract of $\Sigma$ with boundary vertices $\partial\Gamma = \partial\Gamma^- \sqcup \partial\Gamma^+$ satisfying $|\partial\Gamma^-| = m$ and $|\partial\Gamma^+| = n;$
        \item $\mathbf{l} : E \to \mathbb{R}_{>0}$, $e \mapsto l_e$, is an edge-length function such that the boundary leaves $e_v$ incident to $v \in \partial\Gamma$ satisfy $l_{e_v} = L_v.$
    \end{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Equivalence Relation:} Two metric oriented quivers $(\Gamma_1, \mathbf{l}_1)$ and $(\Gamma_2, \mathbf{l}_2)$ representing the same topological surface $\Sigma$ are equivalent if they are related by a finite sequence of \emph{metric edge subdivisions and amalgamations} ($S$-moves), where a subdivision replaces an edge $e$ of length $l_e$ by two edges $e_1, e_2$ with $l_e = l_{e_1} + l_{e_2}$ ($l_{e_1}, l_{e_2} > 0$) joined at a new interior vertex.
    \item \textbf{Composition (Metric Concatenation):} Given $(\Sigma_1, \Gamma_1, \mathbf{l}_1) : \mathbf{L}_1 \to \mathbf{L}_2$ and $(\Sigma_2, \Gamma_2, \mathbf{l}_2) : \mathbf{L}_2 \to \mathbf{L}_3$, the composite morphism $(\Sigma_{12}, \Gamma_{12}, \mathbf{l}_{12})$ is formed by gluing $\Sigma_1$ and $\Sigma_2$ along the common boundary circles. At the level of quivers, for each identified boundary component with perimeter $L$, the outgoing edge $e_1 \in \partial\Gamma_1^+$ and the incoming edge $e_2 \in \partial\Gamma_2^-$ are concatenated into a single edge $e = e_1 \cup e_2$ with assigned length
    \[
    l_e := l_{e_1} + l_{e_2} = 2L.
    \]
    \item \textbf{Monoidal Structure:} Disjoint union of metric circles and disjoint union of metric surfaces.
    \item \textbf{Absence of identities:} The total length $\sum_{e\in E} l_e$ is invariant under $S$-moves, and the total length of a composite is the sum of the total lengths of its factors. Since all lengths are positive, no morphism can act as an identity. Composition is associative, so $\mathbf{MetCob}_2$ is a non-unital monoidal category (a monoidal semicategory), as is usual for bordism categories with metric data.
\end{itemize}
<!-- end def-10 -->
<!-- def-11: body_md -->
The non-unital partial category $\mathbf{HK}^{\text{pre}}$ is defined by:
\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{Objects:} Compact connected Lie groups $G$ (with $G^0 = \{*\}$).
    \item \textbf{Morphisms:} A morphism from $G^m$ to $G^n$ is an isomorphism class of smooth (not necessarily complete) hyperkähler manifolds $(M, g_{\mathbb{H}}, I_1, I_2, I_3, \omega_1, \omega_2, \omega_3)$ equipped with a tri-Hamiltonian isometric action of $G^m \times G^n$ admitting an equivariant hyperkähler moment map
    \[
    \mu = (\mu_1, \mu_2, \mu_3) : M \longrightarrow (\mathfrak{g}^* \times \mathfrak{g}^* \times \mathfrak{g}^*)^{m+n}.
    \]
    An isomorphism between two morphisms is a $G^m\times G^n$-equivariant hyperkähler isometry intertwining the moment maps.
    \item \textbf{Composition:} Let $M \in \operatorname{Mor}(G^m, G^k)$ and $N \in \operatorname{Mor}(G^k, G^n)$. If the diagonal action of $G^k$ on $\mu_{\Delta}^{-1}(0)$ is free and proper, the composition $N \circ M \in \operatorname{Mor}(G^m, G^n)$ is defined by the HKLR hyperkähler quotient
    \[
    N \circ M := (M \times N) /\!\!/\!\!/_{\mathbb{H}}\, G^k := \left( \bigcap_{a=1}^3 \mu_{\Delta, a}^{-1}(0) \right) / G^k,
    \]
    where $\mu_{\Delta, a}(x, y) = \mu_{\mathrm{out}, a}^{(M)}(x) - \mu_{\mathrm{in}, a}^{(N)}(y)$ for $a \in \{1, 2, 3\}$.
    \item \textbf{No identities:} We do not adjoin identity morphisms; see Remark \ref{rem:no-identity} below.
\end{itemize}
<!-- end def-11 -->
<!-- def-12: body_md -->
Applying the Wehrheim--Woodward construction \cite{WehrheimWoodward2010Functoriality} to the composition law of $\mathbf{HK}^{\mathrm{pre}}$, using only non-empty formal sequences (so that no identity morphisms are adjoined), yields the non-unital category $\mathbf{HK}:$
\begin{itemize}
    \item The objects of $\mathbf{HK}$ are finite Cartesian products of connected compact Lie groups $\prod_{i=1}^n G^{(i)}.$
    \item A morphism from $G$ to $H$ is an equivalence class of non-empty formal finite sequences $(M_1, M_2, \dots, M_k)$ of composable basic morphisms, modulo the equivalence relation generated by composing adjacent pairs whenever the intermediate hyperkähler reduction is smooth (see \cite{maiza2026multiplicative,CrooksMayrand2024MooreTachikawa}).
    \item The Cartesian product on objects and morphisms makes $\mathbf{HK}$ a non-unital monoidal category (see also \cite{maiza2026multiplicative,maiza2026lax}).
\end{itemize}
<!-- end def-12 -->
<!-- rem-11: body_md -->
\label{rem:no-identity}
The single-edge spaces $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_e,L)$ cannot serve as identity morphisms. Indeed, by Theorem \ref{thm:hklr-metric-gluing} below, $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_e,L')\circ \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_e,L) \cong \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_e,L+L')$, which is not isomorphic to $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_e,L')$ in $\mathbf{HK}^{\mathrm{pre}}$ by Proposition \ref{prop:cylinder-length}. This is the hyperkähler counterpart of the absence of identities in $\mathbf{MetCob}_2$.
<!-- end rem-11 -->
<!-- thm-8: body_md -->
\label{thm:hklr-metric-gluing}

Let $\Gamma_1$ and $\Gamma_2$ be metric quivers with length assignments $\mathbf{l}_1, \mathbf{l}_2$. Let $v_1 \in \partial\Gamma_1^+$ and $v_2 \in \partial\Gamma_2^-$ be boundary vertices with incident edges $e_1, e_2$ of lengths $l_{e_1}, l_{e_2}$. Let $\Gamma_1 *_{v} \Gamma_2$ be the quiver obtained by identifying $v_1$ and $v_2$ into an interior vertex $v$, and let $(\Gamma_{12}, \mathbf{l}_{12})$ be the quiver obtained by amalgamating $e_1$ and $e_2$ into a single edge $e = e_1 \cup e_2$ of length $l_e = l_{e_1} + l_{e_2}$. 

Then there are canonical hyperkähler isometries
\[
(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_1, \mathbf{l}_1) \times \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_2, \mathbf{l}_2)) /\!\!/\!\!/_{\mathbb{H}}\, G_v \;\cong\; \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_1 *_v \Gamma_2, \mathbf{l}_1 \cup \mathbf{l}_2) \;\cong\; \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_{12}, \mathbf{l}_{12}).
\]
<!-- end thm-8 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->
Let $I_1 = [0, l_{e_1}]$ and $I_2 = [0, l_{e_2}]$. The unglued ambient space of fields decomposes as
\[
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_1) \times \mathcal{B}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_2) = C^1(I_1, \mathfrak{g}^4)^{E_1} \times C^1(I_2, \mathfrak{g}^4)^{E_2}.
\]
Evaluating the diagonal hyperkähler moment map $\mu_{\Delta} = (\mu_{\Delta, 1}, \mu_{\Delta, 2}, \mu_{\Delta, 3})$ at $v$ yields
\[
\mu_{\Delta, a}(A_1, A_2) = A_a^{e_2}(0) - A_a^{e_1}(l_{e_1}) \qquad (a = 1, 2, 3).
\]
Setting $\mu_{\Delta, a} = 0$ for all $a \in \{1, 2, 3\}$ forces exact continuity of the three Higgs fields across the junction:
\[
A_a^{e_1}(l_{e_1}) = A_a^{e_2}(0) \qquad (a \in \{1, 2, 3\}).
\]
Furthermore, the gauge group $G_v$ acts on connection components by $g_v \cdot A_0^{e_2}(0) = \mathrm{Ad}_{g_v}(A_0^{e_2}(0)) - \dot{g}_v g_v^{-1}$. Taking the quotient identifies gauge transformations across the vertex, matching the boundary trivialization sequence
\[
1 \longrightarrow \mathcal{G}_0(\Gamma_1) \times \mathcal{G}_0(\Gamma_2) \longrightarrow \mathcal{G}_0(\Gamma_1 *_v \Gamma_2) \longrightarrow G_v \longrightarrow 1.
\]
By infinite-dimensional Marsden--Weinstein reduction by stages applied to each of the three symplectic forms $\omega_1, \omega_2, \omega_3$ (Theorem \ref{infinite red}) and the quaternionic slice criterion (Proposition \ref{q slice}), the intermediate reduction yields
\[
(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_1, \mathbf{l}_1) \times \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_2, \mathbf{l}_2)) /\!\!/\!\!/_{\mathbb{H}}\, G_v \cong \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_1 *_v \Gamma_2, \mathbf{l}_1 \cup \mathbf{l}_2).
\]
Finally, on $\Gamma_1 *_v \Gamma_2$, $v$ is an interior vertex incident to the edges $e_1, e_2$. The Kirchhoff matching conditions at $v$ are
\[
A_a^{e_1}(l_{e_1}) = A_a^{e_2}(0) \qquad (a=1,2,3).
\]
A pair of $C^1$ solutions $A^{e_1} \in C^1([0, l_{e_1}], \mathfrak{g}^4)$ and $A^{e_2} \in C^1([0, l_{e_2}], \mathfrak{g}^4)$ satisfying these matching conditions, modulo gauge transformations matching at $v$, is uniquely equivalent to a single $C^1$ solution on the concatenated interval $[0, l_{e_1} + l_{e_2}]$ modulo gauge transformations on $[0, l_{e_1} + l_{e_2}]$. The descended $L^2$-metrics agree:
\[
\int_0^{l_{e_1}} \sum_{i=0}^3 \|X_i\|^2 dt + \int_0^{l_{e_2}} \sum_{i=0}^3 \|X_i\|^2 dt = \int_0^{l_{e_1} + l_{e_2}} \sum_{i=0}^3 \|X_i\|^2 dt.
\]
Hence, the identification with $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_{12}, \mathbf{l}_{12})$ is a hyperkähler isometry.
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- thm-9: body_md -->
\label{thm:metric-functor}
The assignment
\[
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbb{H}} : \mathbf{MetCob}_2 \longrightarrow \mathbf{HK}
\]
defined by
\begin{itemize}
    \item on objects: $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbb{H}}\left(\bigsqcup_{i=1}^n (S^1, L_i)\right) := G^n$, with $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbb{H}}(\emptyset) := \{*\}$,
    \item on morphisms: for any metric cobordism $(\Sigma, \Gamma, \mathbf{l})$,
    \[
    \mathcal{Z}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Sigma, \Gamma, \mathbf{l}) := \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma, \mathbf{l}),
    \]
    equipped with its descended hyperkähler structure $(g_{\mathbb{H}}, I_a, \omega_a)$ and boundary hyperkähler moment map,
\end{itemize}
is a well-defined functor of non-unital monoidal categories: it is compatible with composition and with disjoint unions.
<!-- end thm-9 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
We verify the axioms; there are no identity axioms to check, since neither category has identities.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Well-definedness on Morphisms:} By Theorem~\ref{thm:hklr-metric-gluing}, subdividing or amalgamating edges ($S$-moves) preserves the isomorphism class in $\mathbf{HK}$, so $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbb{H}}$ is independent of the representative quiver in the equivalence class.
    \item \textbf{Compatibility with composition:} Let $(\Sigma_{12}, \Gamma_{12}, \mathbf{l}_{12})$ be the composition of $(\Sigma_1, \Gamma_1, \mathbf{l}_1)$ and $(\Sigma_2, \Gamma_2, \mathbf{l}_2)$ along $k$ boundary circles. Concatenating the boundary edges corresponds to hyperkähler reduction by $G^k$ at the identified junctions by Theorem~\ref{thm:hklr-metric-gluing}:
    \[
    \mathcal{Z}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Sigma_2 \circ \Sigma_1) \cong \mathcal{Z}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Sigma_2) \circ \mathcal{Z}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Sigma_1).
    \]
    \item \textbf{Monoidal Multiplicativity:} Disjoint unions of quivers have decoupled Nahm equations and orthogonal $L^2$-metrics, yielding $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Sigma_1 \sqcup \Sigma_2) \cong \mathcal{Z}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Sigma_1) \times \mathcal{Z}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Sigma_2)$.
\end{enumerate}
Thus, $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbb{H}}$ is a functor of non-unital monoidal categories.
<!-- end proof-35 -->
<!-- def-13: body_md -->
The \emph{tropical moduli space} $M_{g,n}^{\mathrm{trop}}$ is the polyhedral cone complex parameterizing isomorphism classes of metric ribbon trees/graphs $(\Gamma, \mathbf{l})$ of genus $g$ with $n$ boundary leaves:
\[
M_{g,n}^{\mathrm{trop}} := \left( \bigsqcup_{\Gamma} \mathbb{R}_{>0}^{|E_{\mathrm{int}}(\Gamma)|} \right) \Big/ \sim_{\mathrm{trop}}
\]
where the disjoint union runs over all combinatorial ribbon graph types $\Gamma$ with $\Sigma(\Gamma) \cong \Sigma_{g,n}$, and $\sim_{\mathrm{trop}}$ is the equivalence relation induced by graph automorphisms and identifying faces when internal edge lengths tend to zero (tropical edge contraction). Here $E_{\mathrm{int}}(\Gamma)$ is the set of edges joining two interior vertices; the lengths of the boundary leaves are fixed by the boundary data.
<!-- end def-13 -->
<!-- thm-10: body_md -->
\label{thm:tropical-bundle}
Fix positive leaf lengths, and let $\Gamma$ be a combinatorial type with $\Sigma(\Gamma)\cong \Sigma_{g,n}$. Let $\mathcal{C}_{\Gamma} \coloneqq \mathbb{R}^{E_{\mathrm{int}}(\Gamma)}_{>0}$ be the corresponding open cone, and for $\mathbf{l}\in \mathcal{C}_\Gamma$ extend $\mathbf{l}$ to all of $E$ by the fixed leaf lengths.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item For every $\mathbf{l}\in\mathcal{C}_\Gamma$, the map $\Psi_{\mathbf{l}}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma,\mathbf{l})\to X(\Gamma)$ of Subsection \ref{subsec:metric-length} is a biholomorphism for $I_1$ which intertwines $\omega_2 + i\omega_3$ with the reduced holomorphic symplectic form, and $X(\Gamma)\cong \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Sigma_{g,n})$ by Proposition \ref{thm:spine-invariance}. Hence the family $\{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma,\mathbf{l})\}_{\mathbf{l}\in\mathcal{C}_\Gamma}$ is identified with the product $\mathcal{C}_\Gamma\times \mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Sigma_{g,n})$ as a family of complex-symplectic manifolds, carrying the fibrewise hyperkähler metrics $g^{X}_{\mathbf{l}}$.
    \item Assume $\dim G > 0$ and $E_{\mathrm{int}}(\Gamma)\neq\emptyset$. For $\mathbf{l}\in\mathcal{C}_\Gamma$ and $\lambda > 0$, let $\mathbf{l}^{\lambda}$ be obtained from $\mathbf{l}$ by multiplying the internal edge lengths by $\lambda$. If $\lambda\neq 1$, then $g^{X}_{\mathbf{l}^{\lambda}}\neq g^{X}_{\mathbf{l}}$. In particular, the hyperkähler metric varies non-trivially along every ray of $\mathcal{C}_\Gamma$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-10 -->
<!-- proof-36: body_md -->
(1) The map $\Psi_{\mathbf{l}}$ is a biholomorphism by Theorems \ref{complex q} and \ref{thm:finite-dim-reduction}. The forms $\omega_2 + i\omega_3$ on $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma,\mathbf{l})$ and the reduced holomorphic symplectic form on $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Gamma,\mathbf{l})$ are both characterized by the property that their pullbacks to $\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma)\subset \mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Gamma)$ coincide with the restriction of the ambient form $\omega_2 + i\omega_3 = \Omega_{\mathbb{C}}$, so $\Phi$ intertwines them; $\varphi$ is a holomorphic symplectomorphism by Theorem \ref{thm:finite-dim-reduction}. The target $X(\Gamma)$ does not depend on $\mathbf{l}$ (Proposition \ref{prop:length-invariance-complex} and Subsection \ref{subsec:metric-length}).

(2) By Proposition \ref{prop:metric-length-dependence}(2), for every $\zeta\in K(\Gamma)$,
\[
g^{X}_{\mathbf{l}^{\lambda}}(\hat{\zeta},\hat{\zeta}) - g^{X}_{\mathbf{l}}(\hat{\zeta},\hat{\zeta}) = (\lambda - 1)\sum_{e\in E_{\mathrm{int}}(\Gamma)} l_e\,\|\zeta_e\|^2.
\]
It therefore suffices to find $\zeta\in K(\Gamma)$ with $\zeta_{e}\neq 0$ for some internal edge $e$. Fix $w\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{C}}\setminus\{0\}$. If $C$ is a cycle, or a path joining two boundary vertices, in the underlying undirected graph of $\Gamma$, set $\zeta_e \coloneqq \pm w$ for $e\in C$, with the sign $+$ if and only if the orientation of $e$ agrees with the direction of traversal, and $\zeta_e \coloneqq 0$ otherwise. At each interior vertex of $C$ one traversed edge enters and one leaves, so their contributions to the Kirchhoff sum cancel, and $\zeta\in K(\Gamma)$. It remains to show that some internal edge lies on such a $C$. Let $e_1$ be an internal edge. If $e_1$ lies on a cycle, or on a path between two boundary vertices, we are done. Otherwise, $e_1$ is a bridge and one of the two components, say $C_1$, of the graph obtained by deleting $e_1$ contains no boundary vertex. Let $w_0$ be the endpoint of $e_1$ in $C_1$. Every vertex of $C_1$ is interior and therefore has degree at least $2$ in $\Gamma$; hence every vertex of $C_1$ other than $w_0$ has degree at least $2$ in $C_1$, and $w_0$ has degree at least $1$ in $C_1$. If $C_1$ has a single vertex, this forces a loop at $w_0$. Otherwise, if $C_1$ were a tree it would have at least two vertices of degree $1$, one of which would differ from $w_0$, a contradiction. In both cases $C_1$ contains a cycle, all of whose edges join interior vertices, i.e.\ are internal. The corresponding $\zeta$ has $\zeta_e = \pm w\neq 0$ on these internal edges, and $\sum_{e\in E_{\mathrm{int}}}l_e\|\zeta_e\|^2 > 0$.
<!-- end proof-36 -->
<!-- rem-12: body_md -->
Theorem \ref{thm:tropical-bundle} describes the family over each open cone of $M^{\mathrm{trop}}_{g,n}$ separately. Passing to $M^{\mathrm{trop}}_{g,n}$ itself requires, in addition, dividing by the automorphisms of $\Gamma$, which act compatibly on $\mathcal{C}_\Gamma$ and on $X(\Gamma)$ by permuting edges, and gluing the families across the faces where internal edges collapse. The behaviour of the harmonic representatives across such faces is described in Proposition \ref{prop:edge-collapse} below; the corresponding statement for the metrics is Conjecture \ref{conj:edge-collapse}. We therefore do not assert the existence of a global smooth fibre bundle over the cone complex $M^{\mathrm{trop}}_{g,n}$.
<!-- end rem-12 -->
<!-- prop-21: body_md -->
\label{prop:edge-collapse}
Let $e_0\in E$ be an edge joining two distinct interior vertices $v_1 = s(e_0)\neq v_2 = t(e_0)$, and let $\Gamma/\{e_0\}$ be the contracted quiver, which is connected with non-empty boundary. Fix all edge lengths except $\ell \coloneqq l_{e_0}\in(0,1]$, and fix the data $(x_e)_{e\in E}$ and the boundary values defining the reduced Kempf--Ness functionals, in a gauge in which the holonomy along $e_0$ is trivial (this can be achieved with the factor $G_{\mathbb{C}}^{v_2}$ of the gauge action), so that the term of $e_0$ in $\bar{F}_\ell$ is $E_{x_{e_0}}(p_{v_1},p_{v_2};\ell)$, with the notation of Remark \ref{rem:rescaled}.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item For every compact $K\subset H$ there is $C_K > 0$ such that, for all $p,q\in K$ and $\ell\in(0,1]$, the rescaled minimizer $\tilde{h}^{*}_\ell$ of Remark \ref{rem:rescaled} and the geodesic $\gamma_{p,q}:[0,1]\to H$ from $p$ to $q$ satisfy
    \[
    \sup_{s\in[0,1]} d_H\bigl(\tilde{h}^{*}_\ell(s),\gamma_{p,q}(s)\bigr) \leq C_K\,\ell^2,
    \qquad
    \Bigl| E_{x_{e_0}}(p,q;\ell) - \frac{d_H(p,q)^2}{\ell} - \ell\int_0^1 V_{x_{e_0}}(\gamma_{p,q}(s))\,ds \Bigr| \leq C_K\,\ell^3.
    \]
    \item Let $p^{*}(\ell)\in H^{\Gamma_{\text{int}}}$ be the unique minimizer of $\bar{F}_\ell$, and let $p^{*}_{\mathrm{col}}$ be the unique minimizer of the reduced Kempf--Ness functional $\bar{F}_{\mathrm{col}}$ of $\Gamma/\{e_0\}$ with the same data. Then $d_H(p^{*}_{v_1}(\ell),p^{*}_{v_2}(\ell)) = O(\ell^{1/2})$, and $p^{*}(\ell)$ converges, as $\ell\to 0$, to the point of $H^{\Gamma_{\text{int}}}$ obtained from $p^{*}_{\mathrm{col}}$ by assigning to both $v_1$ and $v_2$ the value of $p^{*}_{\mathrm{col}}$ at the merged vertex.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end prop-21 -->
<!-- proof-37: body_md -->
(1) Write $x = x_{e_0}$. Choose $o\in H$ and $\rho_K > 0$ with $K\subset \bar{B}(o,\rho_K)$. Closed balls in the Cartan--Hadamard manifold $H$ are geodesically convex and compact, so $M_K \coloneqq \sup_{\bar{B}(o,\rho_K)}V_x < \infty$ and $\gamma_{p,q}$ stays in $\bar{B}(o,\rho_K)$. Testing the rescaled functional on $\gamma_{p,q}$, whose speed is $d_H(p,q)$, gives
\begin{equation} \label{eq:collapse-upper}
E_x(p,q;\ell) \leq \frac{d_H(p,q)^2}{\ell} + \ell\int_0^1 V_x(\gamma_{p,q}(s))\,ds \leq \frac{d_H(p,q)^2}{\ell} + \ell M_K.
\end{equation}
Since $V_x\geq 0$, this yields $\int_0^1\|(\tilde{h}^{*}_\ell)'\|^2\,ds \leq \ell E_x(p,q;\ell) \leq 4\rho_K^2 + M_K$, so the length of $\tilde{h}^{*}_\ell$ is at most $\Lambda_K \coloneqq (4\rho_K^2 + M_K)^{1/2}$, and $\tilde{h}^{*}_\ell$ takes values in the compact convex set $K' \coloneqq \bar{B}(o,\rho_K + \Lambda_K)$. Let $G_K \coloneqq \sup_{K'}\|\mathrm{grad} V_x\|$, which also bounds the Lipschitz constant of $V_x$ on $K'$. By the rescaled Euler--Lagrange equation, $\|\nabla_s(\tilde{h}^{*}_\ell)'\| \leq \frac{\ell^2}{2}G_K$.

Let $f(s) \coloneqq d_H(\tilde{h}^{*}_\ell(s),\gamma_{p,q}(s))$, so that $f(0) = f(1) = 0$. On a Cartan--Hadamard manifold, the distance function $d_H$ on $H\times H$ is convex, smooth away from the diagonal, and its gradient with respect to each argument has norm $1$ there. Hence, wherever $f > 0$,
\[
f'' = \mathrm{Hess}\,d_H\bigl(((\tilde{h}^{*}_\ell)',\gamma_{p,q}'),((\tilde{h}^{*}_\ell)',\gamma_{p,q}')\bigr) + \bigl\langle \mathrm{grad} d_H, (\nabla_s(\tilde{h}^{*}_\ell)', \nabla_s\gamma_{p,q}')\bigr\rangle \geq -\tfrac{\ell^2}{2}G_K,
\]
since $\gamma_{p,q}$ is a geodesic. For $\delta > 0$ let $u_\delta(s) \coloneqq (\frac{\ell^2}{4}G_K + \delta)s(1-s)$, so that $u_\delta'' = -\frac{\ell^2}{2}G_K - 2\delta$. If $f - u_\delta$ had a positive maximum, it would be attained at an interior point where $f > u_\delta \geq 0$, so that $f$ is smooth there and $(f - u_\delta)'' \geq 2\delta > 0$, a contradiction. Letting $\delta\to 0$ gives $f \leq \frac{\ell^2}{4}G_K\,s(1-s) \leq \frac{\ell^2}{16}G_K$, which is the first estimate with any $C_K\geq G_K/16$.

For the second estimate, the length of $\tilde{h}^{*}_\ell$ is at least $d_H(p,q)$, so $\int_0^1\|(\tilde{h}^{*}_\ell)'\|^2 \geq d_H(p,q)^2$ by Cauchy--Schwarz, and therefore
\[
E_x(p,q;\ell) \geq \frac{d_H(p,q)^2}{\ell} + \ell\int_0^1 V_x(\tilde{h}^{*}_\ell(s))\,ds \geq \frac{d_H(p,q)^2}{\ell} + \ell\int_0^1 V_x(\gamma_{p,q}(s))\,ds - \ell\cdot G_K\cdot\frac{\ell^2}{16}G_K.
\]
Together with \eqref{eq:collapse-upper}, this gives the second estimate with $C_K \coloneqq \max(G_K/16, G_K^2/16)$.

(2) For edges $e\neq e_0$, Remark \ref{rem:rescaled} gives $E_{x_e}(p,q;l_e)\geq d_H(p,q)^2/l_e$; for $e_0$ and $\ell\leq 1$, $E_{x_{e_0}}(p,q;\ell)\geq d_H(p,q)^2/\ell\geq d_H(p,q)^2$. Hence, for $\ell\leq 1$,
\[
\bar{F}_\ell(p) \geq D(p) \coloneqq \sum_{e\neq e_0}\frac{d_H(p_{s(e)},p_{t(e)})^2}{l_e} + d_H(p_{v_1},p_{v_2})^2,
\]
and $D$ is proper on $H^{\Gamma_{\text{int}}}$ by the argument of Proposition \ref{proper}. Let $p^{c}\in H^{\Gamma_{\text{int}}}$ be obtained from $p^{*}_{\mathrm{col}}$ by assigning its value $y$ at the merged vertex to both $v_1$ and $v_2$. Using the constant path on $e_0$, $E_{x_{e_0}}(y,y;\ell)\leq \ell V_{x_{e_0}}(y)$, so
\begin{equation} \label{eq:collapse-bound}
\bar{F}_\ell(p^{*}(\ell)) \leq \bar{F}_\ell(p^{c}) \leq \bar{F}_{\mathrm{col}}(p^{*}_{\mathrm{col}}) + \ell V_{x_{e_0}}(y) \leq C_0 \coloneqq \bar{F}_{\mathrm{col}}(p^{*}_{\mathrm{col}}) + V_{x_{e_0}}(y).
\end{equation}
Since all terms of $\bar{F}_\ell$ are non-negative, $d_H(p^{*}_{v_1}(\ell),p^{*}_{v_2}(\ell))^2/\ell\leq C_0$, which is the first claim. Moreover, $\{p^{*}(\ell)\}_{\ell\in(0,1]}\subset\{D\leq C_0\}$, a compact set. Let $\bar{p}$ be the limit of a sequence $p^{*}(\ell_k)$ with $\ell_k\to 0$. Then $\bar{p}_{v_1} = \bar{p}_{v_2}$, so $\bar{p}$ defines a point of $H^{(\Gamma/\{e_0\})_{\text{int}}}$, and by continuity of the convex functions $E_{x_e}(\cdot,\cdot;l_e)$ (Proposition \ref{jgc}) and \eqref{eq:collapse-bound},
\[
\bar{F}_{\mathrm{col}}(\bar{p}) = \lim_{k\to\infty}\sum_{e\neq e_0}E_{x_e}\bigl(p^{*}_{s(e)}(\ell_k),p^{*}_{t(e)}(\ell_k);l_e\bigr) \leq \liminf_{k\to\infty}\bar{F}_{\ell_k}(p^{*}(\ell_k)) \leq \bar{F}_{\mathrm{col}}(p^{*}_{\mathrm{col}}).
\]
Since $\Gamma/\{e_0\}$ is connected with non-empty boundary, $p^{*}_{\mathrm{col}}$ is the unique minimizer of $\bar{F}_{\mathrm{col}}$ (Theorem \ref{kempf ness}), so $\bar{p} = p^{c}$. As every sequence $\ell_k\to 0$ has a subsequence along which $p^{*}(\ell_k)\to p^{c}$, we conclude that $p^{*}(\ell)\to p^{c}$.
<!-- end proof-37 -->
<!-- conj-1: body_md -->
\label{conj:edge-collapse}
In the situation of Proposition \ref{prop:edge-collapse}, the transported hyperkähler metrics $g^{X}_{\mathbf{l}}$ converge, as $l_{e_0}\to 0$ and uniformly on compact subsets of $X(\Gamma)\cong X(\Gamma/\{e_0\})$, to the transported metric of $\Gamma/\{e_0\}$ with the induced lengths. In particular, the pointed hyperkähler manifolds $(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma,\mathbf{l}),\Psi_{\mathbf{l}}^{-1}(x))$ converge in the pointed Gromov--Hausdorff sense, for every $x\in X(\Gamma)$.
<!-- end conj-1 -->
<!-- lem-12: body_md -->
\label{lem 6.10}
Let $x\in\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbb{C}}$ be regular semisimple, and set $c_1 \coloneqq \min\{\|[w,x]\|^2 : w\in\mathfrak{g}^{\perp},\ \|w\| = 1\}$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item $Z(x)$ is a complete, closed, totally geodesic, flat submanifold of $H$, the normal exponential map $\exp^{\perp}:\nu(Z(x))\to H$ is a diffeomorphism, and $V_x$ attains its minimum $\|x\|^2$ at every point of $Z(x)$; moreover $c_1 > 0$.
    \item There is $\epsilon\in(0,1]$ such that, for every unit-speed geodesic $\sigma$ with $\sigma(0)\in Z(x)$ and $\dot{\sigma}(0)\perp T_{\sigma(0)}Z(x)$, one has $\mathrm{Hess}\,V_x(\dot{\sigma}(s),\dot{\sigma}(s))\geq c_1$ for $s\in[0,\epsilon]$.
    \item For every $h\in H$ with $d_H(h,Z(x))\leq\epsilon$, $V_x(h) - \|x\|^2\geq \frac{c_1}{2}\,d_H(h,Z(x))^2$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-12 -->
<!-- proof-38: body_md -->
(1) The right action of $\exp(i\mathfrak{t})$ on $H$ is isometric, and $Z(x)$ is its orbit through $[\mathbf{1}]$. The curves $s\mapsto G\exp(isY)$, $Y\in\mathfrak{t}$, are geodesics, and since $\mathfrak{t}$ is abelian the sectional curvature $-\frac{1}{4}\|[u,v]\|^2$ vanishes on $T_{[\mathbf{1}]}Z(x) = i\mathfrak{t}$; hence $Z(x)$ is the image of the totally geodesic flat $\exp(i\mathfrak{t})\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{rank}\,G}$, which is complete and closed. A closed, complete, totally geodesic submanifold of a Cartan--Hadamard manifold is convex, so its normal exponential map is a diffeomorphism. Since $\tau\in T_{\mathbb{C}}$ centralizes $x$, $V_x(G\tau) = \|x\|^2$, and $V_x$ is invariant under the right action of $T_{\mathbb{C}}$. At $[\mathbf{1}]$ and for $w\in\mathfrak{g}$, decompose $w = w_{\mathfrak{t}} + w^{\perp}$; then $[iw_{\mathfrak{t}},x] = 0$ and $[iw^{\perp},x]$ lies in the sum of the root spaces of $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{C}}$, which is orthogonal to $\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbb{C}}\ni x$ for the Hermitian extension of the inner product. Hence
\[
\frac{d}{ds}\Big|_{s=0}V_x(G\exp(isw)) = 2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\langle [iw,x],x\rangle = 0,
\]
so $[\mathbf{1}]$, and by invariance every point of $Z(x)$, is a critical point of $V_x$. Since $V_x$ is geodesically convex (Proposition \ref{potential}) on the Cartan--Hadamard manifold $H$, every critical point is a global minimum. Finally, $w\in\mathfrak{g}^{\perp}\setminus\{0\}$ does not lie in the centralizer $\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbb{C}}$ of $x$, so $[w,x]\neq 0$; by compactness of the unit sphere of $\mathfrak{g}^{\perp}$, $c_1 > 0$.

(2) By invariance of $V_x$ and of the metric under the right action of $\exp(i\mathfrak{t})$, which is transitive on $Z(x)$, it suffices to treat $\sigma(0) = [\mathbf{1}]$, where $\nu_{[\mathbf{1}]}Z(x) = i\mathfrak{g}^{\perp}$. For a unit vector $n = iw$, $w\in\mathfrak{g}^{\perp}$, let $\sigma_n(s) = G\exp(isw)$. By Proposition \ref{potential} at $s = 0$, $\mathrm{Hess}\,V_x(\dot{\sigma}_n(0),\dot{\sigma}_n(0)) = 2\|[w,x]\|^2\geq 2c_1$. The function $(n,s)\mapsto\mathrm{Hess}\,V_x(\dot{\sigma}_n(s),\dot{\sigma}_n(s))$ is continuous on the compact set $S(i\mathfrak{g}^{\perp})\times[0,1]$, hence uniformly continuous, so there is $\epsilon\in(0,1]$ such that it is at least $c_1$ for $s\in[0,\epsilon]$.

(3) Let $r \coloneqq d_H(h,Z(x))\in(0,\epsilon]$ (the case $r = 0$ is trivial). By (1), $h = \sigma(r)$ for a unit-speed normal geodesic $\sigma$ from the closest point $z = \sigma(0)\in Z(x)$. Set $\psi(s) \coloneqq V_x(\sigma(s))$. Then $\psi(0) = \|x\|^2$, $\psi'(0) = 0$ by (1), and $\psi''(s) = \mathrm{Hess}\,V_x(\dot{\sigma},\dot{\sigma})\geq c_1$ on $[0,r]$ by (2). Integrating twice gives $\psi(r) - \psi(0)\geq \frac{c_1}{2}r^2$.
<!-- end proof-38 -->
<!-- lem-13: body_md -->
\label{lem:transverse_decay}
Let $x\in\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbb{C}}$ be regular semisimple, let $c_1,\epsilon$ be as in Lemma \ref{lem 6.10}, and let $h^{*}\in C^2([0,l],H)$ be the minimizer of $\int_0^l(\|\dot{h}\|^2_H + V_x(h))\,dt$ with $h^{*}(0) = p$ and $h^{*}(l) = q$. Set $d_p \coloneqq d_H(p,Z(x))$, $d_q \coloneqq d_H(q,Z(x))$,
\[
R \coloneqq \max(d_p,d_q,\epsilon), \qquad \lambda \coloneqq \Bigl(\frac{c_1\,\epsilon}{2R}\Bigr)^{1/2} > 0.
\]
Then $\rho(t) \coloneqq d_H(h^{*}(t),Z(x))\leq R$ for all $t\in[0,l]$, and
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:barrier_estimate}
\rho(t) \le \frac{\sinh(\lambda(l - t))}{\sinh(\lambda l)}\, d_p + \frac{\sinh(\lambda t)}{\sinh(\lambda l)}\, d_q \leq \frac{d_p\,e^{-\lambda t} + d_q\,e^{-\lambda(l-t)}}{1 - e^{-2\lambda l}} \qquad (t\in[0,l]).
\end{equation}
In particular, if $d_p,d_q\leq\epsilon$, one may take $\lambda = (c_1/2)^{1/2}$.
<!-- end lem-13 -->
<!-- proof-39: body_md -->
Let $r \coloneqq d_H(\cdot,Z(x))$. By Lemma \ref{lem 6.10}(1), $r$ is smooth on $H\setminus Z(x)$, with $\mathrm{grad}_H r(\sigma(s)) = \dot{\sigma}(s)$ along every unit-speed normal geodesic $\sigma$ from $Z(x)$, and $r$ is geodesically convex, being the distance to a closed convex subset of a Cartan--Hadamard manifold; in particular $\mathrm{Hess}_H r\geq 0$ on $H\setminus Z(x)$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step 1: A differential inequality.} Let $h\in H\setminus Z(x)$, write $h = \sigma(r(h))$ for a unit-speed normal geodesic $\sigma$, and set $\psi(s) \coloneqq V_x(\sigma(s))$. As in the proof of Lemma \ref{lem 6.10}(3), $\psi'(0) = 0$, $\psi''\geq 0$ everywhere by geodesic convexity of $V_x$, and $\psi''\geq c_1$ on $[0,\epsilon]$. Hence
\begin{equation} \label{eq:radial-derivative}
\langle\mathrm{grad}_H V_x(h),\mathrm{grad}_H r(h)\rangle = \psi'(r(h)) = \int_0^{r(h)}\psi''(s)\,ds \geq c_1\min(r(h),\epsilon).
\end{equation}
At every $t$ with $\rho(t) > 0$, $\rho$ is $C^2$ near $t$, and using the Euler--Lagrange equation $\nabla_t\dot{h}^{*} = \frac{1}{2}\mathrm{grad}_H V_x(h^{*})$,
\[
\ddot{\rho}(t) = \mathrm{Hess}_H r(\dot{h}^{*},\dot{h}^{*}) + \langle\mathrm{grad}_H r,\nabla_t\dot{h}^{*}\rangle \geq \frac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{grad}_H r,\mathrm{grad}_H V_x\rangle \geq \frac{c_1}{2}\min(\rho(t),\epsilon).
\]

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step 2: $\rho\leq R$.} If $\rho$ attained a maximum $\rho(t_0) > \max(d_p,d_q)$ at some $t_0\in(0,l)$, then $\rho(t_0) > 0$, so $\ddot{\rho}(t_0)\geq\frac{c_1}{2}\min(\rho(t_0),\epsilon) > 0$, contradicting maximality. Hence $\rho\leq\max(d_p,d_q)\leq R$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step 3: A linear inequality.} For $0 < \rho\leq R$ we have $\min(\rho,\epsilon)\geq\frac{\epsilon}{R}\rho$: if $\rho\leq\epsilon$ this follows from $\epsilon\leq R$, and if $\rho > \epsilon$ from $\rho\leq R$. Hence, by Steps 1 and 2,
\begin{equation} \label{eq:diff_ineq}
\ddot{\rho}(t)\geq\lambda^2\rho(t) \quad\text{at every } t\in(0,l) \text{ with } \rho(t) > 0.
\end{equation}

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step 4: Comparison.} Let $u$ be the solution of $\ddot{u} = \lambda^2u$ on $[0,l]$ with $u(0) = d_p$ and $u(l) = d_q$, namely
\begin{equation} \label{eq:explicit_u}
u(t) = \frac{\sinh(\lambda(l-t))}{\sinh(\lambda l)}\,d_p + \frac{\sinh(\lambda t)}{\sinh(\lambda l)}\,d_q \geq 0.
\end{equation}
Let $w \coloneqq \rho - u$, so that $w(0) = w(l) = 0$. Suppose $w$ attains a positive maximum at $t_0\in(0,l)$. Then $\rho(t_0) > u(t_0)\geq 0$, so \eqref{eq:diff_ineq} applies at $t_0$, giving $\ddot{w}(t_0)\geq\lambda^2 w(t_0) > 0$, which contradicts $\ddot{w}(t_0)\leq 0$. Hence $\rho\leq u$, which is the first inequality in \eqref{eq:barrier_estimate}. For the second, if $0\leq a\leq b$ then $\sinh a\leq\frac{1}{2}e^{a}$ and $\sinh b = \frac{1}{2}e^{b}(1 - e^{-2b})$, so $\sinh a/\sinh b\leq e^{a-b}/(1 - e^{-2b})$; apply this with $(a,b) = (\lambda(l-t),\lambda l)$ and $(a,b) = (\lambda t,\lambda l)$. If $d_p,d_q\leq\epsilon$, then $R = \epsilon$ and $\lambda = (c_1/2)^{1/2}$.
<!-- end proof-39 -->
<!-- cor-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:middle}
In the situation of Lemma \ref{lem:transverse_decay}, for every $\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})$,
\[
\sup_{t\in[\delta l,(1-\delta)l]} d_H\bigl(h^{*}(t),Z(x)\bigr)\leq\frac{(d_p + d_q)\,e^{-\lambda\delta l}}{1 - e^{-2\lambda l}}.
\]
In particular, for fixed endpoints $p,q$, the minimizer converges to $Z(x)$ exponentially fast in $l$, uniformly on the middle portion $[\delta l,(1-\delta)l]$ of the edge.
<!-- end cor-3 -->
<!-- proof-40: body_md -->
For $t\in[\delta l,(1-\delta)l]$, both $t$ and $l - t$ are at least $\delta l$; apply \eqref{eq:barrier_estimate}. Since $\lambda$ depends only on $x$, $d_p$ and $d_q$, it does not depend on $l$.
<!-- end proof-40 -->
<!-- rem-13: body_md -->
\label{rem:long-edge}
Let $e_0$ be a separating internal edge, and consider the limit $l_{e_0}\to\infty$ at a point of $X(\Gamma)$ where $x_{e_0}$ is regular semisimple.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item Lemma \ref{lem:transverse_decay} and Corollary \ref{cor:middle} concern a single edge with fixed endpoint values. In the quiver, the endpoint values $p^{*}_{v_1},p^{*}_{v_2}$ of the global minimizer depend on $l_{e_0}$; bounds on $d_H(p^{*}_{v_i},Z(x_{e_0}))$ that are uniform as $l_{e_0}\to\infty$ are needed to apply them to $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma,\mathbf{l})$.
    \item One expects the Nahm data on the long edge to become asymptotically abelian, with root-space components decaying exponentially away from the endpoints. Deducing this from Corollary \ref{cor:middle} requires, in addition, a bound on the $Z(x_{e_0})$-component of the minimizer that is uniform in $l_{e_0}$ (the operators $\operatorname{Ad}_{\exp(iY)}$, $Y\in\mathfrak{t}$, are unbounded on the root spaces), together with an estimate for the transverse velocity of the minimizer. We do not establish these here.
    \item Let $\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2$ be obtained by cutting $e_0$ at an interior point, so that $\Gamma$ is recovered from $\Gamma_1*_v\Gamma_2$ by an $S$-move. Since $|E_1| + |E_2| = |E| + 1$ and $|(\Gamma_1)_{\text{int}}| + |(\Gamma_2)_{\text{int}}| = |\Gamma_{\text{int}}|$, Theorem \ref{smooth quotient} gives $\dim\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_1) + \dim\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_2) = \dim\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma) + 4\dim G$. Consequently $(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_1)\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_2))/\!\!/\!\!/_{\mathbb{H}}T_G$ has dimension $\dim\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma) + 4(\dim G - \mathrm{rank}\,G)$, which exceeds $\dim\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma)$ when $G$ is non-abelian. A splitting of the form $\lim_{l_{e_0}\to\infty}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma,\mathbf{l})\cong(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_1)\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Gamma_2))/\!\!/\!\!/_{\mathbb{H}}T_G$ therefore cannot hold with these factors. A correct formulation should replace them by moduli spaces of Nahm data on the two halves with a semi-infinite edge and regular semisimple asymptotics, in the sense of Kronheimer \cite{Kronheimer1990coadjoint} (see also \cite{Biquard1996Nahm,Kovalev1996Nahm}), glued along the residual torus symmetry. We leave this to future work.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end rem-13 -->
